# MD Thesis Viva: Master Preparation Plan and Complete Question Inventory

**Thesis:** *Atherogenic Particle-Burden Phenotyping in Familial Hypercholesterolaemia* (MD_SUBMISSION_v3_FINAL.docx). **Candidate:** Dr Nader Genedy. **Prepared:** 27 September 2026.

**What this is.** A plan that leaves no question unaccounted for. The inventory below holds 1031 questions. Every heading of the thesis (154), every table, box and figure (55), every declaration, every one of the 88 paragraphs flagged by the Kit 012 audit (codes K012-001 to 056), every correction found on the paragraph read, every external citation whose number an examiner could test, and the generic questions any MD viva asks. Each row carries: who is likely to ask it (examiner hat), how dangerous it is, where the written answer lives, the evidence to have in hand, and the preparation action.

**Companion documents** (same folder):
- `MD_THESIS_VIVA_FULL_QA_SPOKEN.md/.docx`: full spoken answers with tone and pause cues, by section (rows whose Answer_location begins "MD_THESIS_VIVA_FULL_QA_SPOKEN").
- `MD_THESIS_ANNUAL_REVIEW_VIVA_QA.md/.docx`: the annual-review question bank (rows beginning "MD_THESIS_ANNUAL_REVIEW_VIVA_QA").
- `MD_THESIS_citation_verification.md/.docx`: PubMed verification of the 18 external numbers.
- `MD_THESIS_VIVA_QUESTION_INVENTORY.csv`: this inventory as a spreadsheet with a Status column to tick off.

## 3. Three one-hour mocks

Each mock draws 20 to 25 rows. Ask them in order, time the answers, and score three things: the first sentence (claim or concession), the numbers (slowed and correct), and the stop (no trailing sentence).

**Mock 1, lipid-medicine hat.** S-F1, S-1.1b, S-1.2c, S-2.3a, S-2.3b, S-2.3d, S-4.3a, S-4.6b, S-4.7a, S-5.6c, S-5.7a, S-6.4b, S-6.6c, S-7.7a, S-7.7b, S-7.7d, S-7.7e, S-8.1b, S-8.3c, S-8.6a, S-8.6b, S-X9, S-X10, V-28, V-29.

**Mock 3, genetic-medicine hat.** S-1.3b, S-1.7b, S-3.3c, S-4.2e, S-4.9a, S-5.1a, S-5.2e, S-5.2f, S-5.2g, S-5.3c, S-5.4c, S-5.5c, S-5.6c, S-6.2b, S-7.4a, S-8.4c, A-Q6A, A-Q6B, A-Q6C, A-Q6D, A-Q6E, A-Q6F, A-Q6G, A-Q6H, A-Q6I.

## 5. Rules in the room

1. First sentence: the claim or the concession. Never the background.
2. Numbers: slow, with frame and interval. "1.356 per standard deviation, 1.070 to 1.718, in 1,461 with 75 events."
3. Three phrases to keep ready: "The thesis says so in Section X." "That inference is not permitted by this design." "I will correct that."
4. Never say: validated, external validation (for CALON-5), diagnoses, ascertainment-aware, Index Effect, significant without the interval, 0.756 as transport, 0.718 for diabetes, 0.801 as a result, 38.55 as an effect.
5. Stop when the answer is complete. Silence is the examiner's to fill.
